   223	                writeCommit(target, baseline, plan.text, window)
   224	            }
   225	            InsertionRoute.PASTE -> writePaste(target, baseline, payloadFor(baseline, target.selection))
   226	        }
   227	    }

   229	    private fun writeCommit(
   230	        located: TargetState,
   231	        baseline: String?,
   232	        payload: String,
   233	        window: SurroundingWindow?,
   234	    ): Tick {
   235	        val record = Verification(
   236	            action = AccessibilityInsertionRules.Action.COMMIT,
   237	            beforeText = baseline,
   238	            beforeWasHint = located.read.isShowingHintText,
   239	            selection = located.selection,
   240	            insertedText = payload,
   241	            beforeWindow = window,
   242	        )
   243	        if (expired()) return Tick.Expired(false)
   244	        verification = record
   245	        val outcome = try {
   246	            editor.commit(payload)
   247	        } catch (error: Exception) {
   248	            writeCount += 1
   249	            returned = Returned.THREW
   250	            noteOverrun()
   251	            return judge(record)
   252	        }
   253	        noteOverrun()
   254	        return when (outcome) {
   255	            CommitOutcome.SESSION_CHANGED -> {
   256	                // commitText was never called: the session moved between the check and the write,
   257	                // so the next tick prepares again and will most likely take the paste route.
   258	                verification = null
   259	                Tick.Waiting
   260	            }
   360	    }

   367	
   368	    override fun onAccessibilityEvent(event: AccessibilityEvent?) {
   369	        if (event == null) return
   370	        val remembered = rememberEditableTarget(event)
   371	        updateBubbleFromEvent(event, remembered)
   372	
   373	        // An event from the target's own package can be the editor coming back. That is any other
   374	        // package, or ours when the pinned target is the admitted practice field.
   375	        val eventPackage = event.packageName?.toString()
   376	        if (pendingInsertion != null && (eventPackage != packageName || pinnedTarget?.packageName == packageName)) {
   377	            scheduleRetry(delayMs = 25L)
   378	        }
   379	    }
   380	
   381	    /**
   382	     * Tell the floating bubble whether another app's editable field is active, and where a docked
   383	     * keyboard ends. Event-driven only: nothing here runs at idle, and a windows-changed event caused
   384	     * by our own overlay moving is dropped before any node is touched
   385	     * (`architecture-rules.md` RULE: no-idle-cost, as read by the #135 adjudication).
   386	     */
   387	    private fun updateBubbleFromEvent(event: AccessibilityEvent, remembered: Boolean) {
   388	        val overlay = recordingOverlay ?: return
   389	        when (event.eventType) {
   390	            AccessibilityEvent.TYPE_VIEW_FOCUSED, AccessibilityEvent.TYPE_VIEW_CLICKED -> {
   391	                if (remembered) {
   392	                    // The field is found the direct way; stop any discovery-retry sequence still chasing it.
   393	                    cancelDiscoveryRetries()
   394	                    lastTarget?.let { overlay.fieldActivated(fieldKey(it)) }
   395	                } else if (event.eventType == AccessibilityEvent.TYPE_VIEW_FOCUSED) {
   396	                    // A focus that lands unremembered can be the return to an app whose editor is already
   397	                    // focused (no fresh focus event for the editor itself): discover, and retry until it is
   398	                    // accessible. A click stays cheap (Codex review, BUG 1, 2026-09-13).
   399	                    discoverFieldWithRetry()
   400	                } else {
   401	                    // A click on a non-editor: whether the remembered editor still holds focus is a question,
   402	                    // not a given (a hardware-keyboard tab to a button; Codex round 2). Re-check without a
   403	                    // traversal; if the field is still here, a pending retry sequence has done its job.
   404	                    if (revalidateBubbleField(overlay)) cancelDiscoveryRetries()
   405	                }
   406	            }
   407	            AccessibilityEvent.TYPE_WINDOW_STATE_CHANGED, AccessibilityEvent.TYPE_WINDOWS_CHANGED -> {
   408	                if (event.eventType == AccessibilityEvent.TYPE_WINDOWS_CHANGED && isOwnOverlayWindow(event.windowId)) return
   409	                // A cosmetic windows-changed that finds the field present cancels any pending retry.
   410	                if (discoveryWarranted(event)) discoverFieldWithRetry() else if (revalidateBubbleField(overlay)) cancelDiscoveryRetries()
   411	            }
   412	            else -> Unit
   413	        }
   414	    }
   415	
   416	    /**
   417	     * Whether this window event should run field discovery (one traversal). Always on a window state
   418	     * change; on a windows-changed only when it carries a focus, active, added or removed change, i.e.
   419	     * a genuine app switch or window replacement, never a cosmetic reshuffle. Returning to an app whose
   420	     * editor was already focused emits a windows-changed with the FOCUSED/ACTIVE bit but no state change,
   421	     * which the old state-change-only gate missed and left the bubble hidden (BUG 1, Codex 2026-09-13).
   422	     * ADDED/REMOVED are included because AOSP can expose a returning window with ADDED alone.
   423	     */
   424	    private fun discoveryWarranted(event: AccessibilityEvent): Boolean {
   425	        if (event.eventType == AccessibilityEvent.TYPE_WINDOW_STATE_CHANGED) return true
   426	        if (event.eventType != AccessibilityEvent.TYPE_WINDOWS_CHANGED) return false
   427	        val relevant = AccessibilityEvent.WINDOWS_CHANGE_FOCUSED or
   428	            AccessibilityEvent.WINDOWS_CHANGE_ACTIVE or
   429	            AccessibilityEvent.WINDOWS_CHANGE_ADDED or
   430	            AccessibilityEvent.WINDOWS_CHANGE_REMOVED
   431	        return (event.windowChanges and relevant) != 0
   432	    }
   433	
   434	    /**
   435	     * The bubble's direct route into the session owner: start the owner as a foreground service.
   436	     * Returns false when this process may not do that (no microphone permission, or Android refusing
   437	     * a foreground start from a bound accessibility service), and the caller falls back to the
   438	     * transparent launcher. The focused editor is not pinned here: the owner makes the single pin
   439	     * attempt in `beginSession`, and that result is the record every announcement is judged against (#192).
   440	     *
   441	     * The direct route exists because launching an activity, even a 1x1 non-focusable one, pauses the
   442	     * user's app and Chrome then hides its keyboard (measured on the Android 16 emulator, 2026-09-12).
   443	     */
   444	    fun startDictationFromBubble(request: String): Boolean {
   445	        if (checkSelfPermission(android.Manifest.permission.RECORD_AUDIO) != android.content.pm.PackageManager.PERMISSION_GRANTED) {
   446	            return false
   447	        }
   448	        return runCatching { DictationSessionService.sendCommand(this, DictationSessionService.ACTION_START, request) }
   449	            .onFailure { error -> DebugLogger.warn(TAG, "Direct start from the bubble refused: ${error.javaClass.simpleName}") }
   450	            .isSuccess
   451	    }
   452	
   453	    /**
   454	     * Is the remembered editor still focused? When it is not, and [discover] is set, look for the
   455	     * editor that IS focused right now and adopt it. Discovery is one window traversal, so it runs only
   456	     * on the rare events: a window state change and a service connect, never on the frequent
   457	     * windows-changed stream. Without it a service recreated while a text box already had focus (a Play
   458	     * update, an accessibility toggle) kept the bubble hidden until the user tapped the box again
   459	     * (Codex review of the Play branch, 2026-09-12).
   460	     */
   461	    private fun revalidateBubbleField(overlay: RecordingAccessibilityOverlay, discover: Boolean = false): Boolean {
   462	        var target = lastTarget
   463	        var stillFocused = target != null &&
   464	            runCatching { target.node.refresh() && isSafeFocusedEditor(target.node) && isInFocusedWindow(target.windowId) }
   465	                .getOrDefault(false)
   466	        if (!stillFocused && discover) {
   467	            val found = runCatching { findFocusedEditableTarget()?.takeIf { isInFocusedWindow(it.windowId) } }.getOrNull()
   468	            // Content-free: counts and booleans only (`kotlin-patterns.md` RULE: no-content-in-diagnostics).
   469	            DebugLogger.debug(
   470	                TAG,
   471	                "Bubble discovery found=${found != null} windows=${runCatching { windows.size }.getOrDefault(-1)}",
   472	            )
   473	            if (found != null) {
   474	                clearTarget()
   475	                lastTarget = found
   476	                target = found
   477	                stillFocused = true
   478	            }
   479	        }
   480	        if (stillFocused) overlay.fieldActivated(fieldKey(target!!)) else overlay.fieldLost()

   483	    }
   484	
   485	    /**
   486	     * Discover the focused editor now, and if none is accessible yet, re-look at [DISCOVERY_RETRY_DELAYS_MS]
   487	     * so a returning field that the framework exposes a second or two late still lights the bubble without a
   488	     * re-tap (#141 phone pass). Coalesced per focused window: a stream of windows-changed events for the same
   489	     * return does not restart the schedule (which would postpone the retries forever); a genuinely different
   490	     * focused window starts one fresh sequence. Stops the moment a field is found.
   491	     */
   492	    private fun discoverFieldWithRetry() {
   493	        val overlay = recordingOverlay ?: return
   494	        val focusedId = focusedWindowId()
   495	        // A sequence already chasing this same focused window keeps running; do not restart it, or a burst
   496	        // of events would postpone the retries forever. A still-unknown focused window (-1) coalesces the
   497	        // same way, so a burst during the exact window-unavailable state being repaired does not churn
   498	        // (Codex review, 2026-09-13).
   499	        if (discoveryRetryRunnable != null && focusedId == discoveryWindowId) return
   500	        cancelDiscoveryRetries()
   501	        discoveryWindowId = focusedId
   502	        // Captured BEFORE the first look, so the retry deadlines measure from the transition, not from after
   503	        // a slow initial traversal (Codex proviso, 2026-09-13).
   504	        discoveryStartUptime = SystemClock.uptimeMillis()
   505	        if (revalidateBubbleField(overlay, discover = true)) return
   506	        scheduleDiscoveryRetry(discoveryGeneration)
   507	    }
   508	
   509	    private fun scheduleDiscoveryRetry(generation: Int) {
   510	        if (discoveryAttempt >= DISCOVERY_RETRY_DELAYS_MS.size) return
   511	        val runnable = Runnable {
   512	            // A newer transition or a teardown bumped the generation: this attempt is stale.
   513	            if (generation != discoveryGeneration) return@Runnable
   514	            discoveryRetryRunnable = null
   515	            val overlay = recordingOverlay ?: return@Runnable
   516	            discoveryAttempt++
   517	            if (revalidateBubbleField(overlay, discover = true)) {
   518	                cancelDiscoveryRetries()
   519	                return@Runnable
   520	            }
   521	            scheduleDiscoveryRetry(generation)
   522	        }
   523	        discoveryRetryRunnable = runnable
   524	        // Absolute offsets from the first attempt (uptime timebase), so a slow traversal cannot stretch the
   525	        // schedule past the ~2 s window it is meant to cover.
   526	        mainHandler.postAtTime(runnable, discoveryStartUptime + DISCOVERY_RETRY_DELAYS_MS[discoveryAttempt])
   527	    }
   528	
   529	    /** Invalidate any pending discovery-retry sequence. Bumps the generation so an in-flight runnable that
   530	     *  already left the handler queue sees itself superseded and does nothing. */
   531	    private fun cancelDiscoveryRetries() {
   532	        discoveryGeneration++
   533	        discoveryRetryRunnable?.let { mainHandler.removeCallbacks(it) }
   534	        discoveryRetryRunnable = null
   535	        discoveryAttempt = 0
   536	    }

   538	    private fun focusedWindowId(): Int =

The first grounded mismatch is concrete: H2’s default is not the app’s rendering rule. Cleanup explicitly does not append a period, while smart insertion can capitalize and add a trailing space. I’m now checking the remaining timing assumptions, especially whether History can become terminal before the receipt and whether the driver has a subject-fired capture-ready signal.

    61	    suspend fun finalizeInsertionOutcome(id: Long, status: String, result: String, interrupted: Boolean = false) =
    62	        dao.finalizeInsertionOutcome(id, status, result, clock(), interrupted)
    63	
    64	    /** What one recovery pass closed: the ready rows by id, because each is an insertion outcome to report. */
    65	    data class RecoveredRows(val readyRowIds: List<Long>)
    66	
    67	    suspend fun recoverStaleOpenRows(nowMs: Long, cutoffMs: Long = nowMs - STALE_OPEN_ROW_AGE_MS): RecoveredRows {
    68	        dao.recoverStaleDrafts(cutoffMs, nowMs)
    69	        return RecoveredRows(dao.recoverStaleReadyRowsReturningIds(cutoffMs, nowMs))

     3	import android.content.ComponentName
     4	import android.content.Context
     5	import android.service.quicksettings.TileService
     6	
     7	internal object DictationSurfaceState {
     8	    enum class Phase {
     9	        IDLE,
    10	        LISTENING,
    11	        PROCESSING,
    12	    }
    13	
    14	    private const val PREFERENCES = "dictation_surface_state"
    15	    private const val KEY_PHASE = "phase"
    16	
    17	    fun read(context: Context): Phase {
    18	        val value = context.getSharedPreferences(PREFERENCES, Context.MODE_PRIVATE)
    19	            .getString(KEY_PHASE, Phase.IDLE.name)
    20	        return runCatching { Phase.valueOf(value.orEmpty()) }.getOrDefault(Phase.IDLE)
    21	    }
    22	
    23	    fun update(context: Context, phase: Phase) {
    24	        context.getSharedPreferences(PREFERENCES, Context.MODE_PRIVATE)
    25	            .edit()

    27	            .apply()
    28	        TileService.requestListeningState(
    29	            context,
    30	            ComponentName(context, DictationTileService::class.java),
    31	        )
    32	    }
    33	}
     1	package com.envi.wispr.debug
     2	
     3	import android.content.BroadcastReceiver
     4	import android.content.Context
     5	import android.content.Intent
     6	import android.util.Base64
     7	import com.envi.wispr.paste.PasteAccessibilityService
     8	
     9	/**
    10	 * Debug-only fast eye for the test harness (#181). Hands back the accessibility tree the bound
    11	 * `PasteAccessibilityService` holds, as the XML `uiautomator dump` writes, in the broadcast's result
    12	 * data, so one `am broadcast` replaces a 1.9 s `uiautomator` process start.
    13	 *
    14	 * Result codes are the contract the harness reads:
    15	 *   1  data holds the tree, base64 (base64 keeps the XML clear of `am`'s own quoting)
    16	 *   2  the encoded tree is over [MAX_ENCODED_BYTES]; the harness falls back to `uiautomator`
    17	 *   3  the service is not bound, or reading it threw; the harness falls back and retries later
    18	 *   0  is what `am` reports when NO receiver answered, i.e. a release build; never set here
    19	 *
    20	 * Absent from release builds: this file lives in `src/debug/` and its `<receiver>` is declared only
    21	 * in the debug manifest, gated by `android.permission.DUMP`, which the shell holds and no ordinary
    22	 * app can be granted.
    23	 *
    24	 *   adb shell am broadcast -a com.envi.wispr.debug.DUMP com.envi.wispr
    25	 */
    26	class DebugDumpReceiver : BroadcastReceiver() {
    27	    override fun onReceive(context: Context, intent: Intent) {
    28	        val xml = try {
    29	            PasteAccessibilityService.windowTreeXml()
    30	        } catch (failure: RuntimeException) {
    31	            DebugLogger.error(TAG, "window tree read failed", failure)
    32	            resultCode = RESULT_UNBOUND
    33	            return
    34	        }
    35	        if (xml == null) {
    36	            resultCode = RESULT_UNBOUND
    37	            return
    38	        }
    39	        val encoded = Base64.encodeToString(xml.toByteArray(Charsets.UTF_8), Base64.NO_WRAP)
    40	        if (encoded.length > MAX_ENCODED_BYTES) {
    41	            DebugLogger.warn(TAG, "window tree is ${encoded.length} bytes encoded; over the ${MAX_ENCODED_BYTES} cap")
    42	            resultCode = RESULT_TOO_BIG
    43	            return
    44	        }
    45	        resultData = encoded
    46	        resultCode = RESULT_OK
    47	    }
    48	
    49	    companion object {
    50	        private const val TAG = "DebugDump"
    51	        const val RESULT_OK = 1
    52	        const val RESULT_TOO_BIG = 2
    53	        const val RESULT_UNBOUND = 3
    54	        // The binder transaction buffer is 1 MB and SHARED across in-flight transactions; a quarter of
    55	        // it is the conservative cap (review round 1). A normal screen is ~40 KB encoded.
    56	        const val MAX_ENCODED_BYTES = 256 * 1024
    57	    }
    58	}

  2079	def is_emulator(serial=None):
  2080	    """Whether the driven device is an Android emulator: named like one by adb AND saying so itself.
  2081	
  2082	    The serial prefix alone is what adb assigns, and a caller can hand any string to `WISPR_SERIAL`;
  2083	    `device()` checks only that it is attached. Everything this file does to an emulator (unlock it with
  2084	    a PIN, start a take despite the recording lock) must never reach the founder's phone, so the device
  2085	    is asked `ro.kernel.qemu`, which the emulator's kernel sets to `1` and a phone never does. An
  2086	    unreadable property answers False: the safe side is "not an emulator".
  2087	    """
  2088	    target = serial or device()
  2089	    if not target.startswith("emulator-"):
  2090	        return False
  2091	    try:
  2092	        code, out, _ = _run([ADB, "-s", target, "shell", "getprop ro.kernel.qemu"], timeout=20)
  2093	    except (OSError, subprocess.SubprocessError, UnicodeError):
  2094	        return False
  2095	    return code == 0 and out.strip() == "1"

## Findings

1. **REPRODUCIBLE:** H2’s default expected text is not the app’s deterministic output. Cleanup trims and capitalizes only after existing terminal punctuation; it does not capitalize the first character or append a period (`DeterministicCleanup.kt:516-520`, `DictationSessionCoordinator.kt:1188-1203`). Smart insertion instead capitalizes at a sentence start and adds a trailing space at field end (`InsertionText.kt:52-74,88-95`; proved by `InsertionTextTest.kt:33-38,68-75`). Therefore both `dictate_emulator` and `debug_insert` can reject correct Gmail output under the proposed defaults (`issue-161-2026-09-22-harness-true-verdicts.md:71,94,146`).

   Drop-in plan prose: “H2. `dictate_emulator(sentence, target_package, expected_final, …)` and `debug_insert(text, target_package, expected_final)` require the caller’s literal WHOLE final editor value; omission returns `BLOCKED` before acting. Never derive it from the spoken sentence or `before + text`: the preregistered value includes ASR punctuation and `InsertionText` capitalization/spacing.”

2. **HYPOTHETICAL:** The after-read can fail solely because the harness temporarily paused the editor. Both the recorder launcher and stop/cancel launcher are 1×1, `FLAG_NOT_FOCUSABLE`, and immediately finish (`VoiceInputActivity.kt:79-97`); the pill is also non-focusable (`RecordingAccessibilityOverlay.kt:67-75`). However, the production code records that even this launcher pauses Chrome and hides its keyboard (`PasteAccessibilityService.kt:434-443`). Stop and context cleanup each rely on `_dictation`’s fixed 1.5-second settle (`wispr_eyes.py:1432-1457,1493-1500,1621-1649,1763-1768`). H3 then requires focus again, so a late focus return can produce a false failure (`plan:71-72`).

   Drop-in plan prose: “H3’s unique-focus requirement selects the BEFORE target only. The AFTER read resolves exactly one editor by the saved `(package, resource-id-or-bounds)` identity and reads its whole text even if the harness’s launcher has not yet returned focus. Current focus is reported diagnostically, not made part of the landed verdict.”

3. **REPRODUCIBLE:** T2 polls an asynchronously persisted preference file. `DictationSurfaceState.update` uses `apply()` (`DictationSurfaceState.kt:23-27`), so its in-process listener can observe `LISTENING` before `shared_prefs/dictation_surface_state.xml` is updated. No existing debug receiver exposes the phase; `DebugDumpReceiver` returns only the accessibility XML (`DebugDumpReceiver.kt:9-47`). T2 also does not explicitly establish the device before starting work (`plan:84`).

   Drop-in plan prose: “`run_device_test` calls `device()` first. Remove SharedPreferences-file polling. After each test’s in-process listener observes `LISTENING` and that row’s staging is complete, it calls `Instrumentation.sendStatus` with `driver_phase=READY`; T3 sends it only after B has taken focus. The harness waits for that status in the instrumentation stream, then injects.”

4. **REPRODUCIBLE:** Keeping the three-second warm-up contradicts the subject-fired completion rule (`testing-philosophy.md:85-94`). `LISTENING` is published only after the capture process has admitted and written its first audio block (`AudioCaptureService.kt:595-621`, `DictationSessionCoordinator.kt:763-824`). That is already the capture-ready signal. `SILENCE_STATUS_READY` is the optional auto-stop detector becoming ready later (`DetectorFeed.kt:241-266`), not a prerequisite for injected audio.

   Drop-in replacement for T2’s final sentence: “Inject immediately after the row’s `driver_phase=READY` instrumentation status. Delete the three-second warm-up. Do not wait for `SILENCE_STATUS_READY`; it belongs to optional silence detection, while `LISTENING` already follows the first admitted capture block.”

5. **REPRODUCIBLE:** H8 identifies a process by bare name and may discover an existing recorder only after starting another one (`plan:77`). That contradicts the requirement to identify a process by PID and full command before killing it (`tools-and-apps.md:21-37`). Refusing only when `pidof screenrecord` returns multiple candidates is too late.

   Drop-in plan prose: “Before starting, enumerate screenrecord PIDs and `/proc/<pid>/cmdline`; any existing `/system/bin/screenrecord` is `BLOCKED` with every PID and command named. After starting, require exactly one PID whose full command contains the owned output token. Zero matches, process exit before a non-empty file, or multiple matches fail closed. Send `kill -2` only to that verified PID.”

6. **REPRODUCIBLE:** The revised plan still contradicts its own H9 deletion. H9 deletes `test_dictation` and `dictate` (`plan:78`), but §10 still changes `test_dictation` to `NOT RUN` and counts four remaining suites (`plan:134`). The contract delta still says nonexistent “speaker shell subcommands” (`plan:98`), and the consumer matrix names only one of the three retained activity fixtures (`plan:115`).

   Drop-in fix: At `plan:134`, change “four” to “three” and remove `test_dictation`; at `plan:98`, name `test_dictation` and the `dictate` help/dispatch branch; at `plan:115`, list all three instrumentation fixtures named in §2.2.

## Grounded (G1..G8 found/not found)

- G1: **NOT FOUND as currently designed.** The windows are non-focusable and immediately finish, but the fixed settle remains a hypothetical false-failure seam; Finding 2 removes focus from the after-verdict.
- G2: **NOT FOUND.** The proposed capital-plus-period default contradicts cleanup and smart-insertion code; Finding 1 makes the whole final value explicit.
- G3: **FOUND.** `clear_log()` already executes `logcat -c` (`wispr_eyes.py:2858-2872`). Android logcat accepts `-T` timestamps in `MM-DD HH:MM:SS.mmm` form ([AOSP `logcat.cpp`, lines 443-448 and 532-537](https://android.googlesource.com/platform/system/logging/+/refs/heads/main/logcat/logcat.cpp)); the timestamp is belt-and-braces after the clear.
- G4: **FOUND.** Successful COMMIT finalizes as `status=completed`, `insertionResult=committed`; PASTE uses `pasted` (`PasteAccessibilityService.kt:894-914`, `TranscriptDao.kt:80-91`). For this readable `EditText`, verification occurs after the mutation; the synchronous receipt watcher runs before the verified outcome is enqueued to History (`PasteTargetActivity.kt:57-62`, `PasteAccessibilityService.kt:1405-1413`). No second receipt wait is required.
- G5: **NOT FOUND.** `apply()` makes disk polling the wrong boundary, and `device()` is not explicit. Finding 3 moves readiness onto the instrumentation stream.
- G6: **FOUND.** Device debts use the current transport (`wispr_eyes.py:1728-1731,2216-2219,2355-2366,2891-2901`); only Mac settings use literal `host` (`:2794,2843`). Scenes call the wrappers. `_journal_read()` parses a JSON object, whose keys are strings (`:103-124`), so migration is a locked key move/merge.
- G7: **NOT FOUND as a current live fact.** The AVD encoder/PID/file premise remains unexecuted, which the plan states at `plan:64`; the required live proof remains in `plan:148-149`. `kill -2` is permitted for the shell-owned process, but Finding 5 must establish ownership first.
- G8: **NOT FOUND.** The three-second sleep violates the subject-fired rule, and bare-name `pidof` does not establish process ownership. Findings 4 and 5 close both contradictions.

## VERDICT: PROCEED-WITH-REVISIONS
